Show Scheduled 
For Fairgrounds 


The first annual Contra Costa 
County Sports and Boat Show will 
ibe held May 9 through 12 at the 
County Fair Grounds in Antioch. 

On display will be boats of all 
isizes and descriptions, from the 
smallest run-a-bouts — to family 
eruisers, both’ inboards and out- 
boards. ; 

The show, to be held in the 
mammoth exhibition building on 
the fair grounds, will feature over 
15,000 square feet of boats for 
the casual fisherman and sports- 
man, as well as deluxe specd- 
boats for the most rapid water- 
ski enthusiast. 

Boat dealers from throughout 
Contra Costa County will have on 
display nationally known and ad- 
vertised boats and boating equip- 
ment, and will be on hand to 
answer any questions and furn- 
ish information on any phase of 
the boating sport. 

Outboard motors, ranging from 
the smallest 3.5 horsepower to 
multi-cylinder power plants will 
be available for scrutiny by en- 
gineering-minded fans. 

A special attraction of the show 
is thé large pool which is avail- 
able for all spectators wishing to 
try their skill at the art of fly- 
casting. Spotted throughout the 
show will be exhibitions of pin- 
point fly-casting by local experts. 

Running time of the show, first 
of its kind in central Contra Cos- 
ta County, will be 6-10 p.m. on 
Thursday and Friday, May 9 and 
|10, 10 a.m. to 10 p.m. on Satur- 
day, May 11 and-10 a.m. to 7 p.m. 
on Sunday, May 12.

Stark, Hagstrom 
Tourney Winners 
In Orinda Tennis 


The Junior Round Robin Tennis 
Tournament at Orinda Country 


Club was completed recently 
through a series of individual 
matches. 


The original plans for the tour- 
ney were rained out earlier in 
April. 

The tourney was in two flights, 
one each for boys and girls un- 
der 16. 

Winner of the boy’s flight was 
Dick Stark. 

He was followed by Steve Mil- 
ler, Clint De Witt and Bob 
Hampton in sécond, third and 
fourth places respectively. 

Julie Hagstrom won top honors 
in the girl’s flight. 

Behind Julie were Barbara 
Emerson, Karen Anderson and 
Suzy Ray. 

The tournament was conducted 
on a basis of eight matches of 
six games each, with the win- 
ners compiling the greatest total 


Hardtop Races 


Begin Tomorrow 
At Local Track 


Hardtop drivers will face their 
longest test of the season, 50 lap$ 
when they tangle in tomorrow 
night’s main event at Contra Cos- 
ta Speedway near Walnut Cree} 

Jack London, race director, an- 


nounced that 20 drivers out of an 
expected 100 candidates will qual- 
ify for places on the 50-lap event. 

Record prize money has been 
established in order to lure Nor- 
thern California’s top performers. 

Previous feature races at Con- 
tra Costa have been over the 25- 
lap distance. 

“We were reluctant ‘to hold a 
longer race until the rains stop- 
ped,’’ London explained. 


number of games won through- 
out the tourney. 

Stark and Miss Hagstrom were 
awarded trophies. 

Interest in the tourney was 
high that plans are being readied 
for similar contests throughout 


the summer at the country club”

Page 18 Friday, May 83,1958," 


The Walnut Creek store is: the}Goldman, son of the store’s found-| Planting season is over, you can 
of|still plant roses, for nurseries ev- 


yi /erywhere have. all the popular va- 
rieties of the various rose types 
available in containers. 

Roses in containers are just as 
easily planted as bare root. 

Prepare. the planting hole as 
you do for bare root roses. Cut 
the can away and lift out the plant 
and balled dirt so as not to dis- 
turb the dirt from the roots. 

SET THE PLANT, dirt ball and 

all, into the planting hole, making 
sure the rose will be at the depth 
it was in the container (check the 
dirt line around the trunk.) 
. Center the plant in the hole, 
making sure the rose will have 
room to spread its roots. Fill in 
the hole with a mixture of soil 
and peat moss or compost, then 
water to settle the mixture in 
hole. 

Make a deep, permanent water- 
ing basin around the rose trunk, 
and water regularly and deeply. 

Do not fertilize a newly planted 
rose until it is well established, 
about six months from planting 
time—then use a balanced com- 
mercial fertilizer. 

NO MATTER what rose type 
you want to plant; you will find 
it available in containers. 

The All American selections of 
1957 include. a climber and _flori- 
bunda which every nursery has in 
stock.

Many Varieties of Roses 
Available in Containers 


miniatures, tree and pillar types 
are just as plentiful now in nur 
series as they were during the 
dare root season. 

MARIGOLDS might be callec 
sun-rays, for their brilliant blooms 
of yellow, gold and orange bright: 
a garden as does the sun it- 
self. 

Marigold will do best in rich 
soil with plenty of water and full 
sun; but it will grow in poorer 
soils too. Remove old flowers so 
che plants will bloom for a. long 
period. 

Boxes, pots and other contain- 
ers make fine homes. for mari- 
golds, as do flower beds, where 
you can plant this annual in solid 
masses of a single color or blend- 
ing combinatione. 

Marigolds are handsome cut 
flowers, too. 

RECENT introductions of: new 
varieties have widened the color 
range of the marigold. 

In addition to the standard col- 
ors of yellow and orange, there 
are now bleoms in orange and a 
scarlet sheen, orange with ma- 
hogany centers, apricot, lemon, 
persimmon and cream. 

Marigolds with lar ge r-sized 
blooms and petal variations are 
available now, too. 

Check with your nurseryman on 
his selection of marigolds and 
choose several plants from the 
many varieties available. Mari- 
golds will bring some. cheerful 
Baer sunshine into your gar- 

en.

ing on behind him, on both sides, 
as well as what’s ahead. The 
person who glues his. eyes 
straight ahead when he drives is 
more apt to have trouble than 
the one whd knows the traffic 
pattern all around him,

Garden Memo: 
sooth 

Try Dwarfed 
ye 4 
Citrus Trees 

Here are new suggestions from 
your Garden Checklist: 

1. Try one or several of the 
lwarfed citrus trees as tubbed or 
yatio plants. These ‘trees’ bear 
leavily in tubs and are orna- 
nental all through the year. 

2. Tuberous begonia seedlings 

2an now be set out in that-shady 
100k. The American Hybrid 
strain which had its origin in the 
Santa Cruz area is considered the 
world’s finest. 
_ 8. Once again it’s azalea plant- 
ing time. Various varieties which 
differ in habit and sun tolerance, 
are available. 

4. Remember, when planting 
anything — good soil preparation 
pays off.
